### PL-32: Raw diplomacy labels can leak into popups

**Status:** FIXED (April 12, 2026; audit follow-up added a pause-menu confirmation before `New Campaign` replaces autosave).

**Problem statement**

Proposal and clause display ownership is split across backend and Godot, so raw identifiers such as treaty enums or underscore tokens can leak into popups or degrade wording on fallback paths.

**Confirmed evidence**

- Backend and Godot both keep proposal display mappings.
- `backend/main.py` still formats fallback proposal text ad hoc.
- `backend/game_logic/diplomatic_dialogue.py` rebuilds clause display separately.
- `backend/models/world_state.py` builds counter-offer popup clauses directly from raw clause ids.
- `backend/commands/diplomatic_defiance.py` and `backend/game_logic/ai_diplomacy.py` still own separate formatting paths.

**Root-cause notes**

- Display ownership is split across `backend/display_names.py`, multiple backend helpers, and Godot popup scripts.
- The strongest live raw-leak path is counter-offer popup construction in `world_state.py`, which still builds clauses from raw ids.
- `_include_popup_passthroughs()`, `diplomatic_dialogue.py`, `ai_diplomacy.py`, and sabotage summary code all keep separate fallback formatting logic, so wording can drift even when raw ids do not leak.
- The duplicate Godot proposal-type map is part of the same family and belongs here rather than in a new frontend-only item.

**Exact code surfaces**

- `backend/display_names.py` - canonical display source.
- `backend/main.py` - popup safety-valve formatting.
- `backend/game_logic/diplomatic_dialogue.py` - proposal/clause rendering helpers.
- `backend/models/world_state.py` - counter-offer popup payload construction.
- `backend/commands/diplomatic_defiance.py` - sabotage proposal summary formatting.
- `backend/game_logic/ai_diplomacy.py` - secondary clause display map.
- `godot-client/project-sovereign/scripts/incoming_proposal_popup.gd` - duplicate proposal-type map and underscore fallback.

**Exact failure modes**

- Counter-offer popups can show raw clause ids such as `territory_cede`.
- Proposal type labels can diverge between backend and Godot because both sides keep their own display maps.
- Safety-valve fallback paths can degrade into inconsistent title-casing such as `Open_Borders` or `Non_Aggression`.
- Sabotage and AI proposal summaries can describe the same clause family differently from incoming-proposal popups.

**Edge cases / sibling failure scan**

- Unknown or newly added clause ids should still render through one centralized fallback instead of leaking raw tokens.
- Counter-offer, incoming proposal, sabotage, and fallback popup paths must all be tested together.
- Legacy save data or modded clause ids should degrade consistently through the same formatter.

**State-transition risks**

- Removing the Godot-side map before all backend payloads are normalized can make some popups go blank.
- If one popup path still ships raw ids after the formatter centralization, the bug will survive in a fallback path and be harder to detect.

**Backend / frontend contract risks**

- The backend should ship fully rendered labels plus canonical ids only where machine logic still needs them.
- Godot should render provided display strings, not rebuild labels from ids.

**Acceptance criteria**

- Backend becomes the only owner of human-readable proposal and clause labels.
- Incoming proposal, counter-offer, sabotage, and fallback popup paths all consume the same backend formatter.
- Godot no longer rebuilds proposal labels from enum names or underscore replacement.
- Unknown ids degrade through one centralized fallback formatter instead of leaking raw tokens.
- Popup payload tests fail on raw tokens such as `NON_AGGRESSION`, `territory_cede`, or `Open_borders`.

**Regression test matrix**

- Add backend formatter tests for proposal type and clause rendering.
- Extend popup payload contract tests so raw underscore or enum-style tokens fail.
- Re-run proposal-flow and popup suites after removing the Godot duplicate map.

**Dependencies / blockers**

- Depends on Session 2 transport cleanup so the popup contract is stable before display ownership is collapsed.

**Implementation order inside Session 3**

1. Centralize proposal-type and clause-label rendering in `backend/display_names.py`.
2. Replace backend duplicate formatters in `main.py`, `diplomatic_dialogue.py`, `world_state.py`, `diplomatic_defiance.py`, and `ai_diplomacy.py`.
3. Remove the duplicate Godot proposal-type map and fallback formatting.
4. Re-run popup payload tests, especially counter-offer and sabotage paths.

### PL-28: No defeat-imminent warning before game over

**Status:** FIXED Apr 12, 2026.

**Problem statement**

The player can cross from a damaged position into defeat without any clear "you are about to lose" warning in the notification or dispatch layer.

**Confirmed evidence**

- Current defeat-state rules are already inconsistent enough that the player cannot predict what will end the campaign.
- The playtest loss happened without visible warning.
- The fix must follow the surviving defeat rule after PL-31, not the obsolete capital-loss branch.

**Root-cause notes**

- `turn_manager.py` checks terminal defeat only; it has no near-defeat helper that can emit warnings before the loss condition fires.
- After `PL-31`, the live battlefield defeat rules are "all armies destroyed" and "all territory lost." Time-limit warning already has its own system and should stay separate.
- The current item should not expand into predictive enemy-intent simulation. It only needs a deterministic warning tied to the actual surviving defeat thresholds.

**Exact code surfaces**

- `backend/game_logic/turn_manager.py` - defeat evaluation order.
- `backend/models/world_state.py` - any surviving defeat-threshold tracking.
- `backend/notifications.py` - defeat-imminent notification type.
- `backend/game_logic/dispatch.py` - morning-dispatch warning surfacing.

**Exact failure modes**

- The player can step into terminal defeat with no prior warning when only one army or one region remains.
- Warning wording can drift toward the obsolete capital-loss rule if `PL-31` is not treated as the source of truth first.
- If warning logic mixes in time-limit or enemy-intent prediction, the result will spam or mislead instead of clarifying the live loss rule.

**Edge cases / sibling failure scan**

- Exactly one surviving marshal remains.
- Exactly one controlled region remains.
- The player recovers above the threshold after a warning and should not keep stale warning spam.
- Time-limit warning stays on its separate path and is not merged into this item.

**State-transition risks**

- Warning state must persist long enough to appear in both notifications and the next dispatch, but it must also clear if the player stabilizes.
- The warning should fire before defeat resolution, not after a terminal result has already been returned.

**Backend / frontend contract risks**

- The warning should reuse the existing notification and dispatch surfaces, not create a one-off popup path.
- Wording must match the live defeat rule after the capital-loss branch is removed.

**Acceptance criteria**

- After `PL-31`, a high-visibility warning is emitted when France is down to exactly one living marshal and/or exactly one controlled region.
- The player receives the warning before the live defeat rule fires.
- Warning wording matches the actual surviving defeat condition after `PL-31`.
- The warning appears in both notifications and the following dispatch/readout path while the condition persists.
- The warning clears or stops repeating once the player climbs back above the threshold.

**Regression test matrix**

- Add defeat-warning coverage around the surviving loss threshold.
- Add notification/dispatch assertions so the warning is emitted before the actual defeat result.
- Verify that time-limit warnings are unchanged and remain separate.

**Dependencies / blockers**

- Blocked on PL-31.

**Implementation order inside Session 4**

1. Remove the obsolete capital-loss path via `PL-31` first.
2. Add a deterministic near-defeat helper keyed to one remaining marshal and one remaining region.
3. Wire it into notifications and morning dispatch.
4. Add non-spam coverage for warning persistence and recovery above the threshold.

### PL-26: Combat feels hopeless because the obvious opener teaches the wrong lesson

**Status:** FIXED Apr 12, 2026.

**Problem statement**

The common early "Ney attacks Wellington" line is punishing before the game has taught bombardment, coordination, or setup counters, so the player learns "attacking is hopeless" instead of learning the system.

**Confirmed evidence**

- Repeated attacks in playtest produced defender victories or punishing stalemates.
- Existing audit synthesis says this is primarily a teaching/setup problem, not proof that the combat system lacks depth.
- The current opener surfaces defender stacking before it surfaces viable French preparation lines.

**Root-cause notes**

- The likely first-hour attack line (`Ney` into `Wellington`) presents stacked defensive advantages before the game teaches the counters.
- The old coordination preview is gone, and the first-time coordination tutorial only fires after the player already achieves combined arms.
- The existing bombardment advisory fires only after the player already used artillery correctly.
- This makes the current problem a teaching/order-of-information failure first. Narrow numeric tuning is the fallback only if guidance plus setup still leave the opener feeling hopeless.

**Exact code surfaces**

- `backend/game_logic/combat.py` - modifier surfacing and common-opener outcome messaging.
- `backend/commands/combat_executor.py` - first-time coordination tutorial, bombardment advisory, and any added opener guidance on the attack flow.
- `backend/models/marshal.py` and region/terrain data only if number tuning is still required after surfacing fixes.
- Any tutorial, advisory, dispatch, or wizard surface used to expose the better line.

**Exact failure modes**

- The naive `Ney, attack Wellington` line produces a punishing result before the player is told about bombardment, combined arms, or defender terrain advantages.
- The game teaches combined arms only after success instead of before commitment.
- The post-bombardment advisory is useful but arrives too late to teach the player what to try first.

**Edge cases / sibling failure scan**

- If `Drouot` is unavailable, advice should still surface a non-artillery preparation line rather than naming an impossible move.
- The added guidance should target the common first-hour opener, not spam every later battle.
- Prepared assaults should improve the outcome materially without making all direct attacks trivially safe.

**State-transition risks**

- Guidance added only after the battle result may still be too late if the first failed assault already ends the campaign.
- Broad stat nerfs or buffs could mask the teaching failure while flattening later combat depth.

**Backend / frontend contract risks**

- Reuse existing advisory, objection, tutorial, or result surfaces; this item does not need a new UI system.
- If the advice is conditional, the trigger conditions must stay deterministic enough for regression coverage.

**Acceptance criteria**

- At least one obvious early French preparation line is surfaced as materially better than the naive direct assault.
- The game exposes the key counters behind the Wellington opener before or at the point the player is likely to commit.
- The prepared line is measurably better in the deterministic regression scenario than the naive line.
- Combat depth stays intact; this item does not flatten the system into guaranteed attack wins.

**Regression test matrix**

- Add a deterministic scenario test for the common opener and one prepared alternative.
- If guidance is added to objections, dispatch, or preview text, add a regression that the surfaced advice names the relevant counterplay.
- If narrow number tuning is required, add a regression proving the prepared line improves while the naive unsupported line is still risky.

**Dependencies / blockers**

- No hard code dependency.
- Intentionally sequenced after Sessions 1-3 so crash/defeat/diplomacy noise does not contaminate first-hour tuning.

**Implementation order inside Session 4**

1. Add or restore pre-commit guidance on the common opener attack path.
2. Reuse the existing tutorial/advisory surfaces instead of adding new UI.
3. Build a deterministic naive-vs-prepared comparison test.
4. Only if guidance still leaves the opener hopeless, apply narrow opener-specific tuning and capture it in tests.

---

### PL-29: No supported new-game / restart endpoint

**Status:** FIXED (April 12, 2026).

**Problem statement**

The player still has no clean restart path from the running build. Starting fresh requires server restarts and sometimes manual autosave cleanup.

**Confirmed evidence**

- No formal `POST /new_game` implementation exists in the live backend route set.
- The client pause flow exposes save/load only.
- Existing tests already call `/new_game` indirectly without making it a real supported contract.

**Root-cause notes**

- The backend world is initialized at startup only; there is no reset helper and no restart endpoint.
- The frontend already has save/load wiring, but the pause menu and API client never expose a restart path.
- The pause menu also needs an explicit destructive-action confirmation so one misclick does not immediately replace the current autosave.
- Local client reset logic already exists in the load flow and should be reused instead of inventing a second partial reset path.
- The test suite already assumes `/new_game` exists, so the current state is a direct contract contradiction rather than a speculative feature request.

**Exact code surfaces**

- `backend/main.py` - new-game endpoint wiring and world reset.
- `backend/save_manager.py` - explicit autosave reset/retention behavior.
- `godot-client/project-sovereign/scripts/api_client.gd` - client call.
- `godot-client/project-sovereign/scripts/pause_menu.gd` and `godot-client/project-sovereign/scripts/main.gd` - pause-menu button and UI refresh.

**Exact failure modes**

- Starting fresh requires a process restart and can inherit stale autosave state.
- Existing tests can call `/new_game` even though the route is not supported.
- Frontend local state such as pending popups, dialogue state, or cached world data can leak across a manual restart unless the reset path is centralized.

**Edge cases / sibling failure scan**

- Restart immediately after unsaved play.
- Restart after a manual save/load round trip.
- Restart while popups or dialogues are active.
- Manual saves must remain intact.
- Autosave from the previous campaign must not resurrect stale state after restart.

**State-transition risks**

- Resetting the world must also reset dialogue/mailbox state, notifications, eliminated nations, and any singleton references kept by `backend/main.py`.
- The client must clear local popup/dialogue caches before hydrating the fresh world response.
- Restart and load should share as much UI reset code as possible to avoid parallel bugs.

**Backend / frontend contract risks**

- `/new_game` should return the same kind of hydrated response shape the client already knows how to consume.
- Autosave behavior must be explicit. For the current fix phase, write a fresh autosave immediately after creating the new world so stale autosave state cannot be restored by accident.

**Acceptance criteria**

- `POST /new_game` returns a fresh world state without restarting the process.
- The fresh world is equivalent to a new campaign start: starting regions and marshals restored, `current_turn` reset, no pending diplomacy/dialogue carry-over, eliminated nations cleared.
- Autosave handling on new game is explicit and consistent, and stale autosave state cannot resurrect the previous campaign.
- The pause menu exposes restart/new game and returns the player to a fresh turn-one state.
- The pause menu requires explicit confirmation before restart/autosave replacement.
- Manual saves are preserved.

**Regression test matrix**

- Add formal endpoint coverage in `tests/test_endpoint_wiring.py` or equivalent.
- Add save/load interaction coverage so new-game does not accidentally reload stale autosave state.
- Add a client smoke or manual verification for the pause-menu flow if no Godot harness exists.
- Update or retain the existing `/new_game`-using tests so they now exercise a supported contract instead of an accidental assumption.

**Dependencies / blockers**

- No upstream blocker.
- Keep last in the fix phase because it is QoL, not game-truth or contract-critical.

**Implementation order inside Session 5**

1. Extract a backend world-reset helper that can be used at startup and by `/new_game`.
2. Implement `POST /new_game` and return a fully hydrated fresh-world response.
3. Persist a fresh autosave immediately after reset.
4. Reuse the frontend load-reset path for new-game hydration, then expose the action in the pause menu.
5. Add endpoint, autosave, and pause-flow regression coverage.

## UXR-2 — the layout law (October 12, 2026; Pre-Deploy S9)

> Plan `docs/UX_UI_REVIEW_PLAN.md` §UXR-2 and its landing record; pins `tests/test_uxr2_the_routed_families.py` (Slice A). Found by the reconnaissance and by the new IQ-10 boards (`diorama_rout_conquest`, `diorama_four_contingents`, `diorama_naval`, `war_detail_coalition_{boot,t20}`), which shot states no board had shot.

**UXR-X5's diagnosis, corrected.** The row blamed the tray's cap (scale ≤ 1.0). Measured, the tray scale is exactly 1.0 at all five resolutions, so the cap changed nothing. The cause was the design sizes (10, 11, 12 and 14 px; the 8 belonged to UXR-X6) multiplied by each rank's depth scale `1 − 0.12·depth`, so 10 × 0.88 read 8.8 px on a 1080 panel. Scaling the tray above 1.0 was neither necessary nor sufficient: at 1920×1080 a depth-1 caption still read RED.

**The fix:**
- The depth scale now applies to a block's ART (figures, standard, locket) and not to its TEXT. The text reads at theme sizes at every depth: body 16 for the name, Caption 14 for strength, status, faith, the odometer captions, the skip hint and the reserve tail. Only the text's anchors follow the depth.
- The status line moves under the feet, where faith already sat. At the floor's sizes two lines fit between ranks and three do not.
- `_mk_label` now takes a theme variation, never a size.

**Fixed on the war-detail side (UXR-X6):** the score and both tags are Caption, and the bars are 22 px.

**The reading** (the five diorama boards at five resolutions): RED 84 / P1 151 at HEAD `95ea0459`, shot from a worktree, falls to 0 / 0. The routed six plus the new boards total 55 readings, all at 0 / 0 (`docs/audits/uxr0/readability_routed_2026_10_12.json`).

| ID | Priority | Status | Summary |
|---|---|---|---|
| **UXR-X7** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice A | **At four contingents a side, the next rank's standard covered names, and the reserve tail ran across one.** The four-contingent board (never shot before) showed this at HEAD at every resolution, before any change: Davout's colours over Ney's strength, Charles's over Mack's, John's over Kutuzov's name, Soult's over Lannes's. Two causes. A standard rides its block at +3, so it out-draws the next-inner rank's text. And the tail followed the steps (`base_y − step_y·n − 8`), which at four ranks is the depth-2 rank's name row. **Fixed:** every rank's text draws at `TEXT_Z` 10, above every standard. The tail moves to the baize's outboard bottom corner, over the reserve shelf, which holds nothing at any count. `test_sf7_s9_the_frames.py`'s "the tail follows the steps" pin was flipped consciously. |
| **UXR-X8** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice A | **The war bar's side tags sat under the track's rounded gold cap.** "BR" / "FR" started 3 px inside a bar whose 3-slice cap is 14 px wide, so the first and last glyph were clipped by the rim. This was invisible at 8 px, and the first readable frame showed it. **Fixed:** the tags are inset past the cap (`WAR_BAR_TAG_INSET`). |
| **UXR-X9** | P3 | FIXED October 14, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S12 part 2, part 5e (`d34cbe91`): the verdict is a bounded room ending 6 px above the nameplate (`_fit_verdict_room`), scrolling on whole lines if it must; the IQ-10 board `diorama_verdict_worst` stages the worst case through the production builder (the 137-character template, the Emperor's prefix, the longest named commander's line) and the frame census (`docs/audits/uxr3_s12b/frame_census.json` `verdict_gaps`) reads it three lines and 10 px clear of the plate at every resolution, every tableau ≥ 10 px; the UXR-2 scroll census dropped the verdict from its allowlist; was: owner UXR-3 fixes (Pre-Deploy S12), check R7 "Fit" | **The diorama's verdict can reach the nameplate.** `_verdict` is a `fit_content` RichTextLabel on a free-positioned parent: `_tray_inner` is a plain Control, so it feeds no container minimum, and part 5's census allowlists it on that ground. It starts at y 514; the nameplate's top is ≈ 594, so three lines of IM Fell 17 fit. The captured boards use two. The worst case is the longest observation template (137 characters), plus the sovereign prefix and the speaker wrapper, in an enemy voice: about four lines, landing ≈ 6 px into the plate. **Completion:** the verdict's box is bounded above the plate (the plate rides below the verdict's rendered height, or the verdict scrolls inside a fixed room) and the worst-case text never overlaps it. **Test:** an IQ-10 diorama board staged with the 137-character template and a sovereign speaker, whose machine record shows the verdict's rect ending above the nameplate's. ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S12 · UXR-3 · pillar=ui_ux⟩ |
| **UXR-X10** | P2 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice C | **No open surface refitted when the window or the Interface Scale changed under it.** Every clamped surface called `clamp_centered_panel` on OPEN only, and `main._apply_ui_scale` re-fit the command window alone. So Ctrl+= (which works with a ledger open), the pause menu's slider (under the pause menu's own Settings) or a window resize left an open ledger or popup at its old size, and its header could leave the screen. **Fixed:** one live-refit road. `main._on_root_resized` (window resize and every scale change) calls `DialogManager.refit_visible()` and `top_bar.refit_active_screen()`; each runs `Utils.refit_surface`, which uses a surface's own `refit()` when it has one (the wizard, the diorama, the interrupt and petition popups) and otherwise re-clamps its panel. The main menu refits its open panels on `resized` too, and the war-status panel on `size_changed`. Proven driven: a 1080-px ledger refit inside a 1066-px viewport at scale 1.5 (`tests/test_uxr2_grips_driven.py`). |
| **UXR-X11** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice C | **The Generals screen's filigree corners drifted off the panel.** They were siblings of the PanelContainer at fixed centre offsets that matched only the authored 840×620, so any clamp moved the panel out from under them (32 px at 1600×900 at 1.4), and a grip would have detached them entirely. **Fixed:** they ride a mouse-ignoring `Filigree` Control inside the panel, anchored to its bottom corners with offsets that reproduce the authored look. The layout-law census forbids a centred decoration beside a clamped panel and would have flagged both. |
| **UXR-X12** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice C | **The sizing card's width floor defeated its own clamp.** `custom_minimum_size.x = 560` held the card at 560 px under any logical width below 584 (1600 wide at scale 3.0), so its edges left the screen. **Fixed:** the floor is gone; the authored offsets still give 560 wherever there is room. |
| **UXR-X13** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice C | **A click on the command window's grip froze its default size.** A press and release with no motion stored the current footprint, ending the viewport-fraction default that follows the screen. **Fixed:** only a drag that moved commits (`_grip_moved`), and the shared `ResizeGrip` commits only past a 2-px move. *(The Slice C review found the command window's half incomplete: any motion event, even 1 px of jitter, set `_grip_moved`, and the corner snapped to the cursor with no grab offset. Fixed in the review commit: the press point and grab offset are recorded, and the drag starts only past `ResizeGrip.MOVE_THRESHOLD`.)* |
| **UXR-X14** | P2 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — the UXR-2 Slice C review | **The grip harness reached the player's live backend.** `tools/uxr2_grip_harness.gd` swapped its API stub into `main.api_client`, but main.gd had already handed the REAL client to the top bar in `_ready`. Every screen it opened therefore sent a real GET to `Utils.backend_url()`, which is 127.0.0.1:8005 because the test set no `SOVEREIGN_PORT`: 13 requests per run, inside the commit hook. **Fixed:** the harness also calls `top_bar.set_api_client(stub)` and refuses to run against `:8005`. The test sets `SOVEREIGN_PORT=8993`, a port nothing listens on. |
| **UXR-X15** | P2 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — the UXR-2 Slice C review | **The shared scene parser dropped any node with `groups=[...]` in its header.** `tests/_godot_scenes.py`'s header pattern stopped at the first `]`. A grouped node was lost and its properties folded into the node before it, so a fixed modal rect carrying a group would have passed the layout-law census. **Fixed:** the whole header line is read, the attribute pattern is bracket-aware, and a pin asserts the parser returns as many nodes as each scene has `[node` headers. |
| **UXR-X16** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — the UXR-2 Slice C review | **The layout-law census was more lenient than it claimed.** Three gaps:<br>(1) CHROME read offset deltas as size, so a stretched strip or a container sized by `custom_minimum_size` alone passed as small chrome.<br>(2) The clamp, grip and fitter matches read raw text, so a commented-out call satisfied them.<br>(3) Three of its nine violation codes (SIBLING_DECORATION, UNKNOWN_PANEL_KEY, FITTER_UNREACHED) were never shown failing.<br>**Fixed:** CHROME requires a point on both axes and a positive extent with no larger minimum; the matches read code; a self-test now fails each code. |
| **UXR-X17** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — the UXR-2 Slice C review | **Three small truths about the grips.**<br>(1) The screens' tooltip promised a double-click "restores the size it opened at". It restores the DEFAULT size and forgets the stored one, so the wording now says that.<br>(2) The stored-size ceiling of 3600 logical px stopped a drag short on a 5120-wide panel at scale 1.0, although the store says only the viewport bounds a panel. It is now 8192, a guard against a hand-edited file.<br>(3) The driven "returns on reopen" pin was satisfied by the meta the drag left on the same node, not by the store. The harness now clears the meta before reopening, and a pin holds that `attach_centered` and every open call `load_stored_size()`. |
| **UXR-X18** | P2 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice D | **The war HUD's resize was a hidden 6-px edge band.** It had no cursor and no handle, though its comment promised "drag handles". Its `_input` swallowed any click 0–6 px either side of the panel's left or top edge, on a war card or on the map. Its hit band was computed from the offsets, so it drifted inside the panel whenever the content widened the panel. A drag interrupted by the panel hiding stuck: the next motion resized with no button held. Its width was forgotten on every restart and never re-fit to the viewport. **Fixed:** the band is gone. A visible `ResizeGrip` sits on the panel's top-left corner and persists as `war_status`. The default width is a viewport fraction (12 %, 192–440), the stored height is a cap the content still shrink-wraps under, and the panel refits on `size_changed`. Driven: the grip sits on the corner, a drag widens it, the width survives a hide and show, and a double-click returns the fraction. |
| **UXR-X19** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice D | **The tutor card covered the notice rail its own lessons point at.** Both were anchored to the top-right corner; the card sits on layer 90 over the rail on layer 75. Minimised, its restore button covered the rail's right-aligned icons. **Fixed:** the card sits beside the rail, or below it when there is no room beside it in the right half, and moves when the rail moves or hides. The restore button rides with it. |
| **UXR-X20** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — UXR-2 Slice D | **The war-detail popup was a bare fixed 330×440 rect** (the centre clamp skips an edge-anchored panel). Its header and close button went under the top bar on a logical viewport under 512 px tall. At 800×450 the notice rail covered part of its header, and on the user's 3657-px viewport it stayed a 330-px sliver. **Fixed:** its width and height are viewport fractions (20 % × 55 %, 330–600 × 440–720). It docks a gap left of the war HUD's live edge, centred in the band under the top bar, and is fitted by the new `Utils.clamp_anchored_panel`. It refits on every show and on the live-refit road. |

## UXR-2's extras — S9b (October 12, 2026; Pre-Deploy S9b)

> Plan `docs/UX_UI_REVIEW_PLAN.md` §UXR-2, the S9b landing record; rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §112; pins `tests/test_uxr2b_*.py` and `tests/test_uxr2_grips_driven.py` (TestTheTextSizeSlider). Found while building UXR-D1 (the Text size slider), whose 150 % frames are the first that grew a one-line row past its box.

| ID | Sev | Status | Bug |
|----|-----|--------|-----|
| **UXR-X21** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — S9b Slice A | **The command window's stats line floored its width above the size it was asked for.** The header's stats (`Turn · Actions · Admin · Gold · Inf / Cav / Art`) were one `HBoxContainer` line, 513 logical px at 100 %. The window is a PanelContainer, so it grew to that line whatever its footprint said: 572 px against a requested 537 on a 1080p screen, 601 against 448 on a 1600-px window. The grip's 300-px minimum could never be reached. At Text size 150 % the line reached 726 px and pushed the window to 760 px on a 1080p screen (814 on 1600): 40 % wider. **Fixed:** the stats line is an `HFlowContainer` (h 12, v 2), so its groups wrap under each other. **The wrap exposed what the stats line had been hiding:** the input row's own floor gave the command line itself only its LineEdit default of 4 em. That was about 70 px on an 800-px logical screen and about 100 px at 150 % on a 1080p screen ("Type com"). The command line now holds 8 em (`minimum_character_width`, a theme constant that grows with Text size), about 16 characters at any size. The window's width floor is its input row (413 px at 100 %, 569 at 150 %). It is drawn at the width it was asked for wherever that row fits (537 at 1080p and 100 %, where it had been 572). Driven: `TestTheTextSizeSlider` reads each row's minimum and the drawn width against the requested footprint. |
| **UXR-X22** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — S9b Slice A | **Text size grew the top bar under the HUD that clears it.** The strip is one line of buttons: 36 px at 100 %, 45 px at 150 % and about 50 px at 175 %. Five numbers derive from its authored 36: the rail slot's top at 42, the tutor card's top at 44, the region panel's dock at 48, the command window's height reserve, and both clamps' bands. At 150 % the rail slot sat 3 px under the strip. **Fixed:** `top_bar.gd` publishes the strip's drawn height on its `resized` signal (`Utils.set_top_bar_drawn`), and every one of the five adds `Utils.top_bar_growth()` (0 at 100 %). `bar_height_changed` refits the command window, the tutor card and the region panel, and every clamped surface goes back through the live-refit road. The authored constants and their census pins stand. **And the strip overran the screen:** at 175 % the full-text bar was wider than a 1920-px screen and its nav ran off the left edge, because compact mode switched at a fixed 1,180 logical px. The threshold now scales with the text (`COMPACT_BELOW_PX × Utils.text_size_factor()`), and the bar re-decides compact mode whenever its strip resizes. |
| **UXR-X23** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — S9b Slice B (the user's ruling at Slice A's pause: "go for it" on the recommendation) | **Two controls were called "Text size".** The command window's header had carried a **"Text Size"** label over a stacked + / − since UI-2c (July 12, 2026). It stepped the **Interface Scale**, which grows the chrome with the type. Settings → INTERFACE now carries the real *Text size* (UXR-D1), which grows the type alone. **Fixed:** the header reads **"Scale"** and its action is unchanged (the Ctrl+= / Ctrl+− keys step the same value). Its tooltips say "Interface Scale … panels and text together" and point to Settings → Text size for the words alone. The node names stay, so no path moves. `tests/test_ui2c_global_text_size.py` was re-seated consciously; pinned by `tests/test_uxr2b_hud_band.py::TestTheHeaderSaysScale`. |
| **UXR-X24** | P2 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — S9b Slice B (in the capture harness; **not a game defect**) | **The capture window took the player's keystrokes.** At Slice A, a war-room capture sometimes ended with keyboard focus on the output or a button, not the command line. It happened 1 capture in 3, and the pristine HEAD worktree did it too. **Diagnosed** by logging every focus change (`frame["focus_log"]`) over 8 repeated boots. In the one that failed, focus left the command line at frame 70, long after the boot's last `grab_focus`, and went to the very next control in tab order (the Send button): a Tab press. Shift+Tab gives the output, the control before it. The IQ-10 window is a real OS window, parked past the desktop but foreground when it opens, so keys the player pressed in another program landed in the scene. It also stole the player's keyboard while a capture ran. **Fixed:** `tools/iq10_surface_screenshot.gd` sets `root.gui_disable_input` (the harness never synthesises input; its "presses" emit signals) and counts what still arrives (`frame["stray_input"]`). The war room's own focus path was never at fault. Slice B's regression and its Centred shoot (16 war-room captures) all end with focus on the command line, with 0 stray input. |
| **UXR-X25** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — S9b Slice B | **A zoom still easing in undid the map's re-centre.** Every zoom (the wheel, + / −) runs a 0.2-s tween on the camera's zoom and position. `recenter_view` (Home) and the new ⌂ set the camera at once, and the zoom tween then landed after them and carried the camera back. Found by the driven band harness: + three times, then ⌂ mid-ease, read zoom 0.97 where the fit is 0.90. **Fixed:** `fit_europe` and `recenter_view` kill the zoom tween first. Driven: ⌂ pressed while the zoom eases (0.96) lands on the fit, 0.899 (`tests/test_uxr2b_hud_band.py::TestFitEurope`). |
| **UXR-X26** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — S9b Slice C (the instrument) | **The readability census over-read the map tier by the Interface Scale.** `_add_text_metrics` multiplied every node's canvas scale by the window's `content_scale_factor`. But a node inside a SubViewport has a canvas transform in the SubViewport's own pixels, and the map renders its SubViewport at physical resolution, so the scale was counted twice: 1.4x on the user's panel and 1.5x at 4K. At 4K the old instrument called 24 of 34 labels "not small" when every one was. **Fixed:** for a SubViewport node the multiplier is the physical px its display gives each SubViewport px (`_subviewport_display_scale`: the displaying control's width x the content scale / the SubViewport's width; 1.0 for the map). The BEFORE was re-read through the fixed instrument from the pristine worktree. |
| **UXR-X27** | P3 | FIXED October 12, 2026 — S9b Slice C | **The fleets' sail counts drew at about 2 px.** The count was a child of the ship standee, and the standee scales itself by 64 x 0.85 / 256 = 0.21, so the "11-px" count drew at 2.3 world px. The census read **2.1 px** on the user's panel for all ten fleets: a smudge on every screen since NUI (Sept 23, 2026). **Fixed:** the count is the piece's sibling on the pieces layer, laid out above the hull at the furniture scale, freed with its fleet, outlined like the names. It reads 25 px physical (18 logical) at the whole-map view on the user's panel. |
| **UXR-X28** | P3 | FIXED October 14, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S12b, its head (Slice 0, `4b863aad`; the dock-open board with Slice 3, `2a14b290`). **The instrument first:** the capture harness records every map-tier text row's SCREEN rect (through the SubViewport's canvas transform and the display's offset and scale) and the place names the label layer drew (`frame["map_labels"]`); `tools/uxr3_frame_census.py` reads `map_overlaps` (every pair overlapping by >= 1 px²) and `map_hidden`. **Measured before any fix** on `war_room_boot`'s whole-map view: **71 overlaps** (18 / 12 / 12 / 19 / 10 at 1920x1080, 2560x1440, 3440x1440, 5120x1440, 3840x2160; name/name 22, garrison chip/nation name 18, garrison/name 14, the fogged "?"/name 9 — Masséna's among them). **The fix** (`map_label_layer.gd`, `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §114.6): ONE layout in `FURNITURE_PRIORITY` — the place names keep their place, then the standees' names, then the chips, which nudge within one chip's width or hide at that zoom; a chip that must move prefers a place clear of the pieces; the layout runs once per settled zoom; a name the command window pushes clears the furniture too. **After: 0 at the five resolutions with the dock closed (5 frames) and open (10 frames)** (`docs/audits/uxr_d4/map_census.json`, `tools/uxr_d4_map_census.py`); hidden: chips, and with the dock open at 1920x1080 Mack's fogged name (his "?" and its tooltip stay) — never a standee's name. **Found while measuring, fixed in passing:** a camera moved in code (Home, a focus on a region) left the names drawn at the old view until the next hover (the layer now redraws when the live projection is not the drawn one); a fogged force's name printed its roster key ("ArchdukeJohn"), now the man's name. Test `tests/test_uxr_x28_the_map_furniture.py` (the census on its record, the priority order, the hand-over, the instrument). Frames `docs/audits/uxr_d4/UXR_X28_MAP_FURNITURE_BEFORE_AFTER_{1920x1080,5120x1440}_2026_10_14.jpg` ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S12b · UXR-X28 · pillar=ui_ux⟩ | **At the whole-map view the grown furniture crowds Central Europe.** UXR-X4's floor makes every name, chip and sail count readable at the fit zoom (it was the user's ruling, and the frames are theirs). Where the boot board masses corps it also crowds them: Holland, Hesse and Bavaria. The marshals' names brush the province names, and at Venetia a fogged "?" chip overlaps Masséna's name. The fogged chips and the standees' names are laid out by two different systems, which never saw each other at 6–9 px. Frames: `docs/audits/uxr0/UXR2B_MAP_FURNITURE_BEFORE_AFTER_{5120x1440,1920x1080}_2026_10_12.jpg`. |

## DD-B — the fresh-eyes campaign (October 14, 2026; Pre-Deploy S13; 62 rows DDB-1 … DDB-62, every one homed to the new row S13b "DD-B fixes", the three P1s first; its nine design rows RULED the same day (`DESIGN_REFINEMENT.md` §DD-B), their builds DDB-R1 … R7, the siblings found while ruling DDB-X1 … X4, and two rows brought in from the post-deploy backlog, DDB-X5 … X6, in the second table; **S13b part 1 LANDED October 14, 2026**: DDB-1 … DDB-12 and DDB-X5 FIXED (`08c54539`, `6f360d03`), with the siblings its sweeps found, DDB-X7 … X11 — X7, X8 and X10 FIXED in the part, X9 and X11 head part 2; **S13b part 2 LANDED October 15, 2026**: DDB-X9, DDB-X11, DDB-13 … 20 and DDB-22 … 44 FIXED (`8f3f1141`, `33643c45`, `cd8f3210`), with the siblings its sweeps found, DDB-X12 … X22 — all FIXED in the part but DDB-X17, which heads part 3; **S13b part 3 LANDED October 15, 2026**: DDB-X17 FIXED, DDB-R1 … R7 BUILT and DDB-X1 … X3 FIXED (`eba57ba4`, `ab6314a9`, `89db8acc`), with the siblings its sweeps found, DDB-X23 … X25 — all FIXED in the part)

> Three players who had read nothing played 20 turns each IN THE CLIENT at 5120×1440 (Interface Scale 1.4, a first launch), one road each — conquest, diplomacy-first, the sea — through `tools/dd_b_client.gd` / `tools/dd_b_play.py` against a sandboxed backend (never 8005), and filed every confusion with its frame. Every typed line was read by the ONE judge (`tools/_score_probes.py`; it learned two reply shapes, each checked against every committed census record first); it reads **0 P1**. The write-up is `docs/audits/DD_B_FRESH_EYES_2026_10_14.md`; the records `docs/audits/dd_b/` (`causes.json`, `metric.json`, and per road `rows.json`, `judge.json`, `actions.log`, `summaries.md`, the cited frames); rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §115.
>
> **How these rows were filed.** 96 player rows (34 + 34 + 28) were read against the code (each row's cause is traced to file:line) and de-duplicated across the three roads: one cause sighted several times is one row naming every sighting and its frame. One conquest row was struck as an instrument artifact (the summary cut the opening dispatch's first marshal line; the game lists Massena). A row's severity is the worst of its sightings, as the players graded them. **The metric** (`metric.json`): 69 causes in 60 turns = **11.5 rows per 10 turns** (P1 0.5 · P2 3.5 · P3 7.5); per road conquest 14.0, diplomacy-first 16.0, the sea 12.5. **Done-when (under 5 per 10 turns and 0 P1): NOT met** — so every row is filed and homed to S13b, P1s first. Nothing was fixed in S13.
>
> **The rulings (October 14, 2026, after the close, under the user's delegation: "make design calls please do research and assure the plan has all errors fixed in coming sessions and any similar errors they find while working").** DDB-D1 … D9 are RULED (`docs/audits/DD_B_RULINGS_2026_10_14.md`). D8 and D9's mechanics ride the P1 rows DDB-2 and DDB-3; the other seven builds are DDB-R1 … R7. Four siblings were found while ruling (DDB-X1 … X4). The same day the user brought two items forward from the post-deploy backlog ("i agree"): DW-2, the bilateral scorer's victory weight (DDB-X5), and the naval rail alerts (DDB-X6).
>
> **S13b's parts** (`PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2): part 1 the P1s with D8 / D9 built, then the table's P2s (DDB-1 … 12, X5) · part 2 the table's P3s and the field (DDB-13 … 20, 22 … 44) · part 3 the rulings and their siblings (DDB-R1 … R7, X1 … X3) · part 4 the voice, the client and the sea (DDB-21, 45 … 62, X6). DDB-X4 lands with SFR-B3 at S14's head. **The re-measure** (three fresh players, the same brief and roads) moved on October 14, 2026 to **S14e**, after the new features (VP-1, events, the table lesson), so it reads the game Round 0 will meet; its new rows are filed by DD-B's rule, and if the done-when is not met they become **S14f** before S15.
>
> **The sibling sweep (from S13b on, the user's ruling):** every fix ends with a search for its class — the same mechanism on the other courts, dialogues, popups and roads, and the AI's side of the same executor. Each sibling found is filed here as a found row (DDB-X…, tagged) and fixed in the same slice with its own test; one too large for the slice heads the next part. Nothing found is homed after the deploy.

| ID | Sev | Status | Bug |
|----|-----|--------|-----|
| **DDB-1** | P1 | FIXED October 14, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 1 (`08c54539`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_p1s.py::TestDDB1`; the objection rides the proposal's first response under its `dialogue_id`; the suggestion applies a draft Talleyrand supports and sends nothing; Modify, Cancel and Not Now spend nothing; the band word is the draft's own; the sighting's re-play is DD-B's re-measure, S14e, which reads every road again); was: the decided fix below holds, `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_p1s.py::TestDDB1` pins it, and S13b's closing re-play on the DD-B instrument shows the sighting gone ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **Talleyrand's objection arrives after the proposal has already gone.** The objection is merged into the proposal's dialogue but its popup is queued with no `dialogue_id` (`diplomatic_dialogue.py:1761-1770`), so the response drain holds it back (`main.py:850-851`, `:495-523`). "Use Talleyrand's suggestion" is `send_suggested` (`diplomatic_executor.py:6237-6357`): it charges the DP (`:6292`), puts the proposal in transit (`:6320-6327`) and pauses the running mission (`:6295-6299`); the same response then drains the queued TALLEYRAND OBJECTS popup, and the client's objection route runs before `_display_result` (`main.gd:2925`, `:3891`), swallowing "Talleyrand departs". Modify Proposal then takes the non-dialogue branch (`diplomatic_executor.py:909-913`) and changes nothing; "Not Now" is the relabelled `reconsider` (`proposal_confirm_popup.gd:132-139`, `:259-261`) and drains the same popup. The suggestion regenerates the very terms the objection judges, and the objection's rebuild strips Harsher / More generous / Adjust (diplomacy #31). The Cabinet's "Favorable" on Propose Vassal scores a vassalage with no terms at all (`diplomacy.py:12541-12573`), while the draft it opens demands 15% of the court's treasury a turn ±20% (`diplomatic_templates.py:4134-4138`, `:4178-4183`). **Sightings:** diplomacy-first #23 (P2, turn 6, action 174, `docs/audits/dd_b/diplomacy/frames/0174.jpg`); diplomacy-first #24 (P1, turn 6, action 175, `docs/audits/dd_b/diplomacy/frames/0175.jpg`); diplomacy-first #31 (P2, turn 12, action 258, `docs/audits/dd_b/diplomacy/frames/0258.jpg`); diplomacy-first #32 (P3, turn 12, action 259, `docs/audits/dd_b/diplomacy/frames/0259.jpg`); the sea #20 (P2, turn 10, action 292, `docs/audits/dd_b/sea/frames/0292.jpg`); the sea #26 (P2, turn 16, action 353, `docs/audits/dd_b/sea/frames/0353.jpg`). **Decided fix:** The objection is raised on the proposal before anything is sent: its popup carries the dialogue's `dialogue_id` and stands with the proposal; "Use Talleyrand's suggestion" applies terms he supports (the softened draft he names), and sends nothing until the player sends; Modify returns to the draft with nothing sent and no DP spent; the soften controls survive the objection; "Not Now" closes the proposal without raising the objection; the button's band word is the drafted terms' own. |
| **DDB-2** | P1 | FIXED October 14, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 1 (`08c54539`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_p1s.py::TestDDB2`; DDB-D8 built: a `call_to_arms` letter asks France — Join attaches it to the ally's war, Refuse and two turns unanswered are the offensive refusal at one price, a pair joined at a call may sign a separate peace; the alliance lists the binding and the ally's designs; the series arm C byte-identical (reach 0); the sighting's re-play is DD-B's re-measure, S14e, which reads every road again); was: the decided fix below holds, `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_p1s.py::TestDDB2` pins it, and S13b's closing re-play on the DD-B instrument shows the sighting gone ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **A full alliance carries France into its ally's offensive war without a word.** The offensive cascade puts an ALLIANCE partner at war with the aggressor's target; for France as the callee `_resolve_offensive_call_path` decides alone, with no decision of France's (`diplomacy.py:9795-9976`, `:8794-8839`; the line `display_names.py:953`), so France was put at WAR with Hanover one turn after the general peace. The alliance proposal lists only its clauses (open borders, the payment), never the call-to-arms nor the ally's live design on a court France had sponsored; as a non-leader on Prussia's side France is then refused settlement (`not_side_leader`). **Sightings:** diplomacy-first #29 (P1, turn 11, action 248, `docs/audits/dd_b/diplomacy/frames/0248.jpg`); diplomacy-first #30 (P2, turn 11, action 250, `docs/audits/dd_b/diplomacy/frames/0250.jpg`). **Decided fix:** DDB-D8's ruling, built: an ally's OFFENSIVE war asks France by a letter on the incoming-proposal transport ("Prussia calls on her ally to join her war on Hanover") — Join attaches France to the ally's war instance; Refuse emits the existing offensive-refusal episode; two turns unanswered is a refusal at the same price, said on the letter; a court France joined at an ally's call may be offered a bilateral peace priced by the existing separate-peace penalty (the paradox HARD_STOP lifted for that pair). And the legibility: the alliance proposal states the call-to-arms among its terms ("binds France to Prussia's wars, offensive and defensive") and names every live design of the ally on a court. An AI ally of an AI aggressor and the defensive call are unchanged; BASELINE_SERIES re-measured with an attribution arm and re-pinned with this row named. **Its design half:** `DESIGN_REFINEMENT.md` DDB-D8. |
| **DDB-3** | P1 | FIXED October 14, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 1 (`08c54539`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_p1s.py::TestDDB3`; DDB-D9 built: a truce keeps a serving contingent at its post and out of the Road Home; the peace names and releases it; the series arm T byte-identical (reach 0); the sighting's re-play is DD-B's re-measure, S14e, which reads every road again); was: the decided fix below holds, `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_p1s.py::TestDDB3` pins it, and S13b's closing re-play on the DD-B instrument shows the sighting gone ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=vassals⟩ | **A client's contingent garrisoning a conquest marches home, unordered, when France's war only pauses.** `shared_enemies` counts only WAR states (`contingent.py:118-124`): an armistice with Britain is not WAR and Austria was at peace, so France and Holland shared no enemy and Dumonceau's contingent took the road home at once (`contingent.py:771-809`, `world_state.py:3038-3050`), leaving Ulster ungarrisoned, with no word before the truce was accepted; the treaty's withdrawal message then counts the contingent's own march home as "your own orders" and reports him "not moved" (`withdrawal.py:818-827`, `:968-970`; `contingent.py:96`). **Sightings:** the sea #19 (P1, turn 10, action 274, `docs/audits/dd_b/sea/frames/0274.jpg`); the sea #21 (P2, turn 11, action 302, `docs/audits/dd_b/sea/frames/0302.jpg`). **Decided fix:** DDB-D9's ruling, built: for a contingent's purposes a pair in ARMISTICE is still the war it shares (the raise stays WAR-only), so a truce keeps it at its post and a peace — or the eight homeward turns — releases it; a serving contingent is exempt from the Road Home's issuance and judging during its lord's truce; every lord alike (Golden Rule 5). And the legibility: the PEACE preview and a court's peace offer name every contingent the peace releases and the post it leaves; the withdrawal message recognises a contingent's march home ("marching home under Holland's colours"), never "not moved". **Its design half:** `DESIGN_REFINEMENT.md` DDB-D9. |
| **DDB-4** | P2 | FIXED October 14, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 1 (`6f360d03`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_table.py::TestDDB4`; "Let Talleyrand haggle (1 DP)"; the dry run decides before the charge — none, and the offer stands with nothing spent; the war score re-read at every show; the sighting's re-play is DD-B's re-measure, S14e, which reads every road again); was: the decided fix below holds, `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_table.py::TestDDB4` pins it, and S13b's closing re-play on the DD-B instrument shows the sighting gone ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **"Counter" on a court's offer sends terms the player never sees, and can end the offer.** Counter is a one-click automatic counter (`diplomatic_executor.py:7861-7928`, `ai_diplomacy.py:2809-2858`): it charges 1 DP, then `generate_counter_offer` builds terms or returns None (also when the court cannot pay its own DP, `:2853-2857`); on None the offer is popped with "found our counter-terms unacceptable". The popup's war score is the snapshot taken when the offer arrived (`ai_diplomacy.py:2775-2782`). **Sightings:** conquest #10 (P2, turn 4, action 83, `docs/audits/dd_b/conquest/frames/0083.jpg`); the sea #12 (P2, turn 5, action 100, `docs/audits/dd_b/sea/frames/0100.jpg`). **Decided fix:** The button says what it does ("Let Talleyrand haggle — 1 DP") and its tooltip what it may cost; a dry run decides before anything is charged — no counter possible, the offer stays and nothing is spent; the popup's war score is re-read whenever it is shown. |
