| **DDB-X11** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`8f3f1141`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX11`; `WAR_AGE_PENALTY_MAX` 30 → 65; the AI's send floor re-read at the shipped weight (`ai_diplomacy._send_floor`: an honest truce or peace letter's floor falls by (0.65 − 0.3) × |war score|); the fifteen isolations lifted, each pin named; the series arms A and F byte-identical); was: the fix below holds, `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX11` pins it, the seam-isolated pins read at the shipped weight, and BASELINE_SERIES re-measured (re-pinned with this row named if it moves) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **At DW-2's ×0.65 a two-turn war cashes out again, and a beaten court's bare letter can fall under its own send filter.** Found by DDB-X5's sweep (every pin calibrated in the old band): CA9 row 1's war-age penalty (`WAR_AGE_PENALTY_MAX` 30, `diplomacy.py:250`) was sized against the ×0.3 credit, so at ×0.65 its arm A re-opens at war score +30 (`tests/test_ca9_row1_war_age_penalty.py::TestTheCheeseIsClosed`); and an AI P1 truce or peace letter to a winning France now scores under the AI's send filter (< 20) — on the 1805 boot no P1 letter is sent at either weight (coalition loyalty), so its reach is narrow. Fifteen pins (five of `test_da1_ai_intelligence`, three of `test_nation_agendas`, two of `test_session4_diplomacy`, three of `test_settlement_gate4_leg1_fixes`, one of `test_sf_nav1_d1_the_tilsit_clause`, CA9 arm A) are seam-isolated at the pre-DW-2 ×0.3 (`bilateral_war_score_weight`) with this row named. **Decided fix:** scale `WAR_AGE_PENALTY_MAX` by the credit ratio (30 × 0.65 / 0.3 ≈ 65) and re-read arm A at the shipped weight; re-read the AI letter's thresholds at the shipped weight (the bilateral's war-age and harshness terms beside the table's, one demand-side reading) so a beaten court's honest letter is sent; lift every isolation; re-measure the series. |
| **DDB-X12** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`8f3f1141`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX12`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX12` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **A court's bilateral peace letter titled the status quo at the signature and never listed it.** Found by DDB-X9's sweep (every surface that states the status quo): the incoming bilateral peace letter (`mailbox_payloads.attach_peace_preview`) showed the terms but not who keeps what, where France's own outgoing preview did. **Decided fix:** `diplomatic_dialogue.attach_status_quo_forecast` is the ONE reader for France's preview and the letter, re-read at every show. |
| **DDB-X13** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`33643c45`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX13`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX13` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **A harsher round could not be stepped back.** Found by DDB-30's sweep (every escalating round): "Less generous" restored the previous generous round, but a harsher round had no way back. **Decided fix:** The harsh variant offers "Milder terms" (no "harsh" in its label, so a typed "harsh" keeps meaning harsher), restoring the previous round exactly. |
| **DDB-X14** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`33643c45`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX14`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX14` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **The advice quoted the stored war score.** Found by DDB-27's sweep (every quoted war score): the advisory's lines read `world.war_scores`, a turn stale beside the live reading. **Decided fix:** The advice quotes `live_war_score_for`, the assessment's own read. |
| **DDB-X15** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`33643c45`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX15`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX15` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **The overview and the war-room counsel used the ladder word with no direction.** Found by DDB-25's sweep (every ladder-word sentence): "Military: overmatched" and "Their overmatched military position" did not say who outmatches whom. **Decided fix:** `diplomatic_advisory.strength_sentence` — "we outmatch them" / "they outmatch us" — on every strength line. |
| **DDB-X16** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`33643c45`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX16`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX16` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **The armistice quote and the per-court chip promised a counter by band alone.** Found by DDB-26's sweep (every band-word reader). **Decided fix:** `counter_aware_likelihood` — ONE read: the band promises a counter only when the generator's dry run builds one. |
| **DDB-X17** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 3 (`eba57ba4`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX17`; `combat_executor.charge_on_peace_soil` / `world_state.reckless_charge_peace_owner`: the player's charge — typed, answered or fired by an attack's reckless stage — is refused onto peace soil at no cost in the attack's own words, no popup first; the reckless auto-charge picks no target there; the AI keeps PT-F1; twelve WO-24 / WO-25 and sibling pins re-read with this row named; BASELINE_SERIES byte-identical); was: the fix below holds, `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX17` pins it, the WO-24 / WO-25 pins it moves are retuned with this row named, and BASELINE_SERIES re-measured ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=combat_legibility⟩ | **The glorious charge, and the reckless auto-charge, can still take the field on soil France is at peace with.** Found by DDB-14's sweep (every road by which the player's corps takes a field): DDB-14 refuses the attack's lead (`field_on_peace_soil`), but `_execute_glorious_charge` and `_process_reckless_cavalry_turn_start` keep only PT-F1's frontier rule (the charge fights, the province does not transfer). Applying the refusal at the charge's door in part 2 moved eight WO-24 / WO-25 pins (the charge's frontier halts), so it was reverted and homed here. **Decided fix:** the player's charge (typed or reckless) reads `field_on_peace_soil` before it rides: a charge onto peace soil is refused at no cost and names the court, as the attack is; a reckless auto-charge picks no target on peace soil; the WO pins re-read the rule with this row named. |
| **DDB-X18** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`cd8f3210`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX18`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX18` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=living_balance⟩ | **The reckless auto-charge alarmed Europe for a win on France's own soil.** Found by DDB-42's sweep (both combat copies): `_process_reckless_cavalry_turn_start`'s mirrored pipeline added `battle_win` / `decisive_victory` on any field. **Decided fix:** The mirror reads `battle_win_on_own_soil` (the series arm B holds both copies down). |
| **DDB-X19** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`cd8f3210`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX19`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX19` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=living_balance⟩ | **A province passing between two other courts by treaty, or by the auto-charge's conquest, went unannounced.** Found by DDB-43's sweep (every road that changes a province's controller: the capture, the auto-charge's mirrored conquest, the treaty cession, the ask-back return — already a dispatch line — the homeland return, vassal grants and formations — their own events). **Decided fix:** `announce_third_party_capture` at the treaty cession and at the auto-charge's conquest (arm D; 16 third-party changes announced on the 40-turn sim, byte-identical). |
| **DDB-X20** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`cd8f3210`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX20`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX20` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=living_balance⟩ | **A treaty handing a court back its own province charged +8 `treaty_annex`, as for an annexation.** Found by DDB-42's sweep (every alarm on a change of hands): the cession loop counted every transferred province as annexed. **Decided fix:** `world_state.cession_recovers_province` — a province ceded back to its 1805 holder or its title's holder adds no `treaty_annex` (series arm Y, byte-identical; reach none on the sim). |
| **DDB-X21** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`cd8f3210`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX21`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX21` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=marshal_drama⟩ | **The cautious objection's alternative read one corps against one marshal.** Found by DDB-19's sweep (every attack-ratio reader): `DisobedienceSystem._get_strength_ratio_for_alternative` picked retreat / fortify / defend on the solo ratio while the objection itself read the muster. **Decided fix:** It reads `muster_expected_totals` too. |
| **DDB-X22** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 2 (`cd8f3210`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX22`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX22` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=combat_legibility⟩ | **The glorious charge's banner said "a devastating cavalry assault" over a charge that was thrown back.** Found by DDB-17's sweep (every line chosen before the outcome): the executor's banner was fixed text, and the resolver's line claimed "devastating damage". **Decided fix:** The banner follows the outcome ("leads the cavalry in!" when beaten); the resolver's line states the mechanic in no outcome's register ("strikes at double weight — and takes double in return"). |
| **DDB-X23** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 3 (`ab6314a9`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX23`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX23` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=combat_legibility⟩ | **A committed muster on a corps two provinces off was retired as "marched away", and the attack never went in.** Found by DDB-R2's sweep (the widened gate now pauses cavalry leads): `strategic.muster_is_live` judged the target live only on or next to the lead, so a range-2 cavalryman's Commit read "has marched to …" from a muster that had not moved (it re-routed RS-27's arm at turn 7). **Decided fix:** the live check reads the lead's reach (`movement_range`), the attack's own range rule; RS-27's arm then plays HEAD's world over all forty turns. |
| **DDB-X24** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 3 (`ab6314a9`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX24`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX24` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=command⟩ | **The player's bare "attack" with nobody in reach marched a non-literal marshal a step at once.** Found by DDB-R1's sweep (every road by which an attack becomes a march): `_attack_nearest_target` asked a literal marshal ("Shall I pursue?") and moved every other one toward the nearest foe, 1 AP, unasked — R1's class. **Decided fix:** the typed bare attack asks in R1's words when the pursuit can be paid; otherwise (one action left, or any other road) the step stands. |
| **DDB-X25** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — Pre-Deploy S13b part 3 (`89db8acc`; `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX25`); was: the fix below holds and `tests/test_ddb_s13b_the_rulings.py::TestDDBX25` pins it ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13 · DD-B · pillar=combat_legibility⟩ | **The help's garrison entry said a detachment "Fights to destruction."** Found by DDB-X2's sweep (every surface that describes a garrison): the help predated SR-7a's surrender floor and DDB-R3's capitulation. **Decided fix:** it names both — "it lays down its arms below 500, and capitulates whole to an assault at 4:1 odds." |

## CC — "Click & Command" (October 15, 2026; row CC, `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13c; 10 rows CC-X1 … CC-X10 found while planning, building and capturing CC-0 / CC-1; **CC-0 + CC-1 LANDED October 15, 2026**: CC-X1 … X5 and X10 FIXED; **CC-2 LANDED October 15, 2026**: CC-X8 FIXED; X6, X7 and X9 OPEN (CC-4), each homed to the CC part that owns its code)

> The user asked for better ways to command troops "using combos of clicks and writing", picked all four offered features, and named the edge case: "avoiding edge cases like overwriting commands when you click something". The exploration found that edge case — and its neighbours — already live on the client; the plan (`~/.claude/plans/sorted-yawning-clover.md`, approved October 15, 2026) filed them here before any feature was built. Pins: `tests/test_cc_click_and_command.py` (driven through `tools/cc_client_harness.gd` on the real `main.tscn`, real keys via `root.push_input`).

| ID | Sev | Status | Bug |
|----|-----|--------|-----|
| **CC-X1** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — row CC, CC-0 (`tests/test_cc_click_and_command.py::TestTypingAfterAMapClick`; the control arm proves the hotkey still ends the turn on an empty line) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-0 · pillar=command⟩ | **Typing after a map click fired hotkeys — `e` ended the turn.** `map_renderer_base.gd` releases the command line's focus on every left/middle press (pinned there), and nothing gave it back, so the next bare key reached `_unhandled_input`: `Soult, march to `, a click on the map, then `e` ENDED THE TURN; L/T/G/D/R/N opened screens, Tab hid the terminal, M/+/− moved the map. **Fixed:** type-through (`main.gd::_input`) — with a draft on the line, a printable key, Backspace, Delete or Enter goes to the draft; the arrows still pan and an empty line keeps every hotkey. |
| **CC-X2** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — row CC, CC-0 (`::TestAnOfferNeverOverwritesADraft`) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-0 · pillar=command⟩ | **A dispatch TODAY chip (and the tutor's) wrote over a half-typed order; a relayed tail over a draft was silently lost.** `_on_tutorial_suggest_command` set the whole line; `_fill_pending_relay` skipped a non-empty line and the tail was retired at the next send. **Fixed:** an offered order waits at the head of the suggestion row (↳); Tab or a click takes it and PARKS the draft (↩), Tab again swaps back, and a parked draft returns to the line when control does. A held relay keeps its CR-7-8 "sent unchanged" flag. |
| **CC-X3** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — row CC, CC-0 (`::TestTheLatchAndTheWire::test_a_chip_after_the_warning_disarms_it`; source pin `::TestEverySenderRetiresTheLatch`) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-0 · pillar=command⟩ | **The end-turn warning, then any chip, then a bare Enter ended the turn.** Only `_execute_command` disarmed `_awaiting_end_turn_confirmation`; the twelve chip and answer senders did not, so a chip order sent after the warning left the latch armed under an empty line. **Fixed:** every sender calls `_retire_line_latches()` before it sends (the envoy-review answers excepted by the warning's own design); the census pin reds a new sender that forgets it. |
| **CC-X4** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — row CC, CC-0 (`::TestTheLatchAndTheWire::test_a_panel_chip_waits_for_a_command_on_the_wire`) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-0 · pillar=command⟩ | **A province-panel chip posted behind a typed command still on the wire.** The panel is not a modal and its chips checked only the chip latch — not `command_input.editable`, the rail's UX23-A guard — so a click queued a second POST behind the first. **Fixed:** the guard, and the rail's own sentence. |
| **CC-X5** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — row CC, CC-1 (`::TestTheRowAndTheMap::test_the_piece_in_front_wins_the_hover`) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-1 · pillar=ui_ux⟩ | **The tooltip (and now the click) named the piece BEHIND the one under the cursor.** Two ranks' hit boxes overlap and `_refresh_hover_state` took the first box in array order, not the y-sorted piece drawn in front. **Fixed:** the box whose bottom is lowest — the front piece — wins. |
| **CC-X10** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — row CC, CC-0 (`::TestTheNextOrdersKeysLand`; reproduced on the untouched client `04e2348b` with a headless probe before the fix) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-0 · pillar=command⟩ | **After an order was sent with Enter, the next order's keys were dropped until the player clicked the line.** Found by CC's own Mode C capture: Godot 4.4's LineEdit leaves EDITING on submit (`keep_editing_on_text_submit` defaults off) and KEEPS focus, so `set_input_enabled(true)`'s `grab_focus()` — a no-op on a control that already holds focus — re-armed nothing; the line looked live and ate every key. **Fixed:** the line keeps editing on submit; control return calls `_resume_editing()`; and the safety net in `_on_command_input_gui_input` switches editing back on when a text key reaches the focused line on any path (the signal runs before the LineEdit's own handler). |
| **CC-X6** | P3 | OPEN — owner row CC, CC-4's head (`PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13c part 4); completion: the tail is relayed and named like every other tail, pinned in `tests/test_cc4_chained_orders.py`, and corpus row `parseneg-until-then-attack-holds` re-pinned with its reason ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-4 · pillar=command⟩ | **`hold … until Davout arrives then attack` silently drops the attack.** HOLD and SUPPORT never read `attack_on_arrival` (`strategic_parser.py:1140-1148`); the order is accepted and the typed clause does nothing, with no word said. |
| **CC-X7** | P2 | OPEN — owner row CC, CC-4's head (`PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13c part 4); completion: Compromise keeps the typed condition merged as whichever-first, pinned in `tests/test_cc4_chained_orders.py` ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-4 · pillar=command⟩ | **Answering a marshal's objection with Compromise discards the condition the player typed.** The order is rebuilt with only `max_turns` / `auto_cancel_below_ratio` (`strategic_executor.py:2334-2346, 2385-2404`); Insist keeps it. |
| **CC-X8** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — row CC, CC-2 (`tests/test_cc2_march_here.py::TestARefusedMarchLeavesTheSquare`, with its sensitivity arm: the old order put back breaks the square on the same refused march) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-2 · pillar=command⟩ | **A march order breaks the square before its refusal checks run.** `_auto_break_square` (`strategic_executor.py:802`) runs ahead of the engaged / road refusals, so a march the game then refuses has already changed the corps' formation. **Fixed:** the break runs at the start of stage 7 (`_strategic_objection`), once every refusal of stages 2–6 has passed; the objection reads the broken square exactly as before, and stages 3–6 read no square state. |
| **CC-X9** | P3 | OPEN (unverified) — owner row CC, CC-4's sibling sweep of the standing orders (`PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13c part 4); completion: reproduced or struck with the reason, and if real fixed with a pin ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13c · CC-4 · pillar=command⟩ | **A PURSUE order outlived its destroyed quarry.** The user's live campaign, turn 6: the Orders tab showed Napoleon "Pursue Archduke John — tracking" a turn after John's corps was destroyed and removed from `world.marshals`; `_execute_pursue` completes on a missing target, so either the order did not run at turn start or the ledger read stale data. |

## PC — the peace conundrum report's defects (October 15, 2026; `docs/audits/PEACE_CONUNDRUM_2026_10_15.md`; homed to `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13d; **S13d LANDED October 15, 2026**: PC-F1, F3, F5 and F6 FIXED, with the siblings its sweeps found, PC-X1 … X4 — all FIXED in the part; rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §120; pins `tests/test_pc_peace_conundrum_fixes.py`; the report's B–E expansions stay the user's to rule)

| ID | Sev | Status | Bug |
|----|-----|--------|-----|
| **PC-F5** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13d (`tests/test_pc_peace_conundrum_fixes.py::TestPCF5`; the series attributed, `tools/_pc_series_arms_final.json` — arm 0 reproduces DD-B's series byte for byte and every arm is byte-identical — `BASELINE_SERIES` does not move; the seam was met on 29 of 35 AI phases, 68 pair scores; the commanded CMD-H campaign moves from turn 11 by this seam alone — Austria's armistice envoy, reading its live score, now arrives a turn before Britain's coalition settlement offer — attributed in `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §120) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13d · PC · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **The AI judges a war by last turn's score.** The AI diplomatic phase (`turn_manager.py:547`) runs before `advance_turn`'s `recalculate_war_scores` (`:589`, `diplomacy.py:10750`): a beaten Austria at −73 asked only for an armistice because it read −54, and it sued a turn later than its own rule says. **Fixed:** `turn_manager.ai_phase_reads_the_field` recalculates the stored scores just before the phase, on the end-turn road; the advance recomputes again after its pruning and ticking. The sweep: the enemy military phase reads no stored score; every AI diplomatic reader runs inside the phase. |
| **PC-F6** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13d (`::TestPCF6`, `::TestPCF6TheSaveRepair`) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13d · PC · pillar=combat_legibility⟩ | **A repulsed assault that bled the garrison 3:1 was booked as a decisive AUSTRIAN victory.** `_resolve_garrison_combat` records a holding garrison as `defender_victory` (`combat_executor.py:5061-5069`) and `record_battle` credits the decisive exchange to `winner_nation` without asking who bled (`diplomacy.py:10631`): Massena's 8,485 : 2,872 at Vienna took the war's last decisive slot from France. **Fixed — the ruling:** a decisive is the winner's only when the winner INFLICTED the exchange (`battle_scale.is_decisive_victory_exchange` / `decisive_credit`, the reading the threat arms already made); a victory at 2:1 cost and a bloody repulse book none; **a garrison that holds still books a defender victory** (the city stood; the blood component prices the dead). A pre-fix save's misbooked decisive is dropped on load where its battle row proves it (the live campaign's Vienna record). The sweep's siblings are PC-X3 and PC-X4; bombardment records through the same `record_battle` (checked). |
| **PC-F1** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13d (`::TestPCF1`; five older pins re-seated to the new words) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13d · PC · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **"Would break an ally's war" never names the ally.** The wizard chip (`diplomacy.py:12714`, `:13041`), the counsel, the Congress levers and the typed refusal leave the player to discover that Bavaria is the block, or that a downgrade below DEFENSIVE_ALLIANCE lifts it. **Fixed:** ONE wording, `diplomacy.paradox_refusal_words` — "Bavaria is still at war with Austria — propose an armistice, or first Downgrade the alliance with Bavaria (2 steps) below a defensive alliance" — on the wizard rows (WAR and ARMISTICE pages), the send road's refusal and `can_sign`'s verdict (the HARD_STOP display, which printed raw keys), the typed refusal, the per-court chip, the counsel ("Mark one thing, Sire: …"; it no longer recommends the refused peace) and the Congress lever (PC-X1). Every ally is named; no client copy hard-codes the refusal. |
| **PC-F3** | P2 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — `PRE_DEPLOY_PLAN.md` §2 S13d (`::TestPCF3`) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13d · PC · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **The war panel's score is not the one the peace math reads.** The panel said "vs Britain · 71 · Harsh Peace" (every pair of the war, under the leader's name); the settlement verdict read the equal-weight side pressure 17 — tier white peace, a −10 legitimacy charge — and the stored score of the turn's start. **Fixed:** every war row carries `peace_table` (`war_status._peace_table`): the pressure the scorer reads, each court's total out of 50 for a settlement with no terms (the preview's own verdict), and — when the field has moved but the table has not — "This turn's battles and captures count from next turn: the table reads 17 until the turn ends; the field stands at 24." `settlement_tier` names the scorer's tier and its side follows the same number; the HUD tooltip and the detail popup print the lines. |
| **PC-X1** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — found by PC-F1's sweep, fixed in S13d (`::TestPCF1::test_the_congress_peace_lever_reads_the_send_road`; the sign-ability sweep's records re-taken, A = 0 and B = 0 on all ten arms; `congress:signed` falls where the paradox closes the lever, FC 6 → 2) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13d · PC · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **The Congress priced a "sign a peace" lever the send road refuses.** `congress._lever_verdict` asked `can_sign` without `at_send`, so the alliance paradox (a send-road clause) never closed the lever: "sign a peace with Austria" was offered while Bavaria's war made it a hard stop. **Fixed:** a PEACE lever reads the send road; the closed lever quotes PC-F1's refusal. |
| **PC-X2** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — found by PC-F1's sweep, fixed in S13d (`::TestPCF1::test_the_typed_refusal_names_both_ways_and_the_truce_honestly`) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13d · PC · pillar=diplomacy⟩ | **The typed refusal promised a truce's expiry "makes the peace".** It does so only at relations of −60 or better (`ARMISTICE_AUTO_PEACE_RELATION`; the report's F7). **Fixed:** the refusal says so. ## CODE-X1 — the docs caps read the checkout's line endings (October 15, 2026; found while landing S13d from a clean worktree; **FIXED the same day**; rules `CODE_HEALTH_PLAN.md` §The ratchet pin; pins `tests/test_code_health_ratchet.py::TestTheByteCapsReadNoLineEndings`)

| ID | Sev | Status | Bug |
|----|-----|--------|-----|
| **CODE-X1** | P3 | FIXED October 15, 2026 — found landing S13d (`40a3f7bc`) from a clean worktree, fixed in a side session (`tests/test_code_health_ratchet.py::TestTheByteCapsReadNoLineEndings`: a CRLF and an LF copy of both docs read the same, the CRLF copy reads the census's own reading, and both copies really differ on disk; reproduced on a fresh sparse worktree at `4645de5f` — HEAD's ratchet failed there at 62,329 B — and green there with the fix; the sensitivity arm, the raw `st_size` put back, fails three pins) ⟨SF step=pre-deploy S13d · CODE-X1 · pillar=none⟩ | **The `CLAUDE.md` and `STATUS.md` byte caps failed in any fresh Windows worktree, even at HEAD.** The ratchet (`test_claude_md_stays_on_its_diet`, `test_status_md_stays_on_its_diet`) and the docs diet (`test_code4_docs_diet.py::test_status_under_the_cap`) read `st_size`, and the repo runs with `core.autocrlf=true`: a fresh checkout writes CRLF, so `CLAUDE.md` weighed ~430 B more than the same commit LF in the main checkout (62,329 B against 61,904 B at `4645de5f`; the S13d session measured 62,383 against 61,958) and the 62,000 cap failed. **Fixed:** `_code_health_census.text_bytes` reads the size with CRLF folded to LF (IQ-8's `lf_bytes` rule for the driver revision), `doc_bytes` reads both capped docs through it, and the docs diet's STATUS pin reads it too; the caps and the "cap near its reading" pin keep their values, set at the LF reading. **The sweep (Golden Rule 10):** every other `st_size` / `getsize` reader in `tests/` and `tools/` is a binary file (the PNG frames, the March `.pck`), an emptiness check (`test_naval_diorama.py`, `release_smoke.py`'s log) or a generator's printout; every hash of a checked-in text file reads `read_text` (newline-normalised) or `lf_bytes`; the raw-byte readers in `stage_fn.py`, `retire_lever.py` and `speak_handlers.py` read raw on purpose, to write the file back with its own endings. No sibling. |

## UXR-3 — the review (October 13, 2026; Pre-Deploy S11; 84 rows: UXR3-1 … UXR3-73 for every ✗ and UXR3-F1 … F11 found while reading; S12 part 1's re-read closed 45 of the 73, part 2's closing re-read 24 more and F1 … F10; 4 re-homed to UXR-4 at S15 with their frames and the readers' reasons; F11 is S14's; each row says which)

> The look-and-feel review: 145 surfaces × the eight items of `docs/UX_CHECKLIST_V1.json` (frozen at `603d983f` before the first reading), read on that tree by the instruments and three blind readers. Review `docs/audits/UXR3_REVIEW_2026_10_13.md`; records `docs/audits/uxr3/`; rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §113; pins `tests/test_uxr3_checklist_frozen.py`, `tests/test_uxr3_the_review.py`.

**How these rows were filed** (the checklist's own filing rule):
- **Every ✗ is a row's surface and item.** 373 ✗ across 138 surfaces, in 73 rows. The ✗s of one item with one cause on several surfaces of one scene share a row, which names every surface and its frame. A surface the capture harness has never shot gets one row for all of its items, with the missing shot as its first fix.
- **The owner is S12, "UXR-3 fixes".** A row's completion is its item re-read ✓ on each named surface, against the same frozen checklist and the same tools.
- **Severity.** P2 for a player-visible item (R1, R2, R3, R5, R7, R8) on a surface of the top five groups. P3 otherwise, and for R4 (a source census) and R6 (a missing driven proof) everywhere. No crash and no SCRIPT ERROR was found, so there is no P1.
- **The spread.** A reader item carries its spread (3–0 or 2–1); a 2–1 ✗ is filed like a 3–0 ✗.
- **Found while reading (F1 … F11).** These are the readers' R4 notes and the conductor's checks: defects no item's mark rests on, each verified on its frame or carried by notes from two or three readers. F11 is a backend COPY row; the plan routes it to the ending pillar's owner (S14), never to UXR.
- **Already owned, cross-referenced and not re-filed:** UXR-X9 (the diorama's verdict can reach the nameplate) and UXR-X28 (the map's furniture crowds Central Europe). Both are in §UXR-2 above; X9 was fixed at S12 part 2, X28 at S12b's head (October 14, 2026).
- **The frames** are under `docs/audits/uxr3/frames/`: each surface's own panel, cropped from the 1920×1080 frame unless the row names another resolution, never scaled.

Frames: `docs/audits/uxr3_s12b/frames/UXR3_TOP_BAR_BOOT_1920x1080.jpg`, `docs/audits/uxr3_s12b/frames/UXR3_TOP_BAR_BOOT_5120x1440.jpg`. The readers' reasons: top_bar_boot R2 (3–0): The Cabinet chip 'Talleyrand: Idle' is a small grey chip at the far right, the same size as Envoys and the nav buttons, and the red 'Threat 70 / 100' beside it pulls the eye first / The 'Talleyrand: Idle' Cabinet chip is neither first nor largest: it sits mid-right, smaller than the blank Envoys chip, and the red Threat figure and the nav row pull the eye first. The decided fix, if the user's mark is ✗: none to the bar's order. The board's must_show is IQ-4's wording ('Talleyrand: Idle', never 'None'), which reads on the frame, and one chip in a row of same-size peers cannot be the largest element; if the user wants it to lead, the Cabinet chip moves to the head of the bar's right-hand cluster, before the Admiralty chip (`top_bar.tscn`) | **`top_bar.tscn` — R2 Hierarchy fails on 2 surface(s).** `top_bar_boot` (3–0): reader 1: The Cabinet line 'Talleyrand: Idle' is small plain text in the right cluster; the eye goes to the boxed nav buttons on the left, the red [THREAT!] and the Envoys box, not to it. / reader 2: 'Talleyrand: Idle' is a small plain-text field at the far right, the same size as DP and Turn, and the eye lands first on the nav tabs and the red [THREAT!] beside it. / reader 3: 'Talleyrand: Idle' is small plain text in the right-hand cluster, no larger than DP or Turn; the eye goes first to the boxed nav buttons on the left and to the red [THREAT!] beside it.. Frame: `docs/audits/uxr3/frames/UXR3_TOP_BAR_BOOT_1920x1080.jpg`.<br>`top_bar_mission_court` (3–0): reader 1: 'Talleyrand: Courting → Denmark' is plain small text squeezed between the red [THREAT!] and the gold-lit Envoys (2) button, which take the eye instead. / reader 2: 'Talleyrand: Courting → Denmark' is body-size plain text squeezed between the red [THREAT!] and the gold-bordered Envoys (2) button, which takes the eye instead. / reader 3: 'Talleyrand: Courting -> Denmark' is small plain text, and the gold-boxed 'Envoys (2)' and the red [THREAT!] next to it pull the eye away from it.. Frame: `docs/audits/uxr3/frames/UXR3_TOP_BAR_MISSION_COURT_1920x1080.jpg`. |

Frames: `docs/audits/uxr3_s12b/frames/UXR3_LEDGER_MISSION_TRANSIT_FORCES_1920x1080.jpg`, `docs/audits/uxr3_s12b/frames/UXR3_LEDGER_MISSION_TRANSIT_FORCES_5120x1440.jpg`, `docs/audits/uxr3_s12b/frames/UXR3_LEDGER_MISSION_STARVED_FORCES_1920x1080.jpg`, `docs/audits/uxr3_s12b/frames/UXR3_LEDGER_MISSION_STARVED_FORCES_5120x1440.jpg`. The readers' reasons: ledger_mission_transit_forces R2 (3–0): The surface's point, the mission PAUSED while he carries a letter, appears nowhere: the Cabinet block reads an active 'COURTING +8 a turn' with a live [Recall], the opposite of must_show / The Cabinet block reads 'COURTING … +8 a turn' with a [Recall] link; nothing in the frame says the mission is paused while he carries a letter, so the thing the shot is for is absent; ledger_mission_starved_forces R2 (3–0): No line says the mission is suspended for want of DP: the Cabinet block reads an active 'COURTING +8 a turn … 2 DP a turn', so the surface's point is absent / The Cabinet block reads an active COURTING mission with '4 funded turns'; no line says the mission is suspended for want of DP. The decided fix, if the user's mark is ✗: re-stage both boards in `tools/iq10_capture_payloads.py`, then re-shoot: their payloads carry `paused: false`, so the client's paused branch (warning colour, no Recall, already in `strategic_ledger.gd` `_cabinet_block`) never ran. The transit board's proposal is refused at the backend's own gate (Sweden at −60 where Open Borders needs −20), so Talleyrand never leaves; it is to send one the gate passes. The starved board's mission did not pause after the DP write (`paused: false`, '4 funded turns'); the staging is to find what re-funds it within the end turn and write the shortfall after it, and the payload's `facts` must read `paused: true` before the board is shot.
